A single public entrypoint\n``run_eval(spec, ...) -> EvalOutcome`` executes the seven-step sequence:\n\n    1. build isolation  (HomeIsolation.setup)\n    2. deploy hooks     (deploy_hooks_to)\n    3. spawn            (executor.spawn)\n    4. inject           (executor.inject)\n    5. observe          (executor.observe)\n    6. assert           (each ExpectCallable applied to an EvalContext)\n    7. teardown         (HomeIsolation.teardown)\n\nand maps the outcome to a DM-001 ``EvalOutcome`` (T03.01) following the\nstatus-mapping rules documented in ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md``:\n\n* All applied ``ExpectCallable``s returned ``passed=True`` -> ``PASS``.\n* At least one ``ExpectResult.passed`` is ``False`` and no harness\n  exception fired -> ``FAIL``.\n* Any exception other than ``KeyboardInterrupt`` / ``SystemExit`` raised\n  during steps 1-6 -> ``ERRORED`` with ``error_class`` = fully-qualified\n  exception classname.\n* ``KeyboardInterrupt`` / ``SystemExit`` propagates so NFR-REL1\n  (T03.07) can mark the outcome ``INTERRUPTED`` at the orchestrator\n  layer. Internal state + classification helpers\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass _LifecycleState:\n    \"\"\"Mutable per-call state captured by ``run_eval``.\n\n    Kept private to the module — the public surface is ``EvalOutcome``.\n    The dataclass groups everything the classification helper needs so\n    the body of ``run_eval`` stays close to the seven-step structure\n    without smuggling positional results through ``return`` tuples.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    spec: EvalSpec\n    observed: ObservedRun = _NO_OBSERVED_RUN\n    expects: list[ExpectResult] = field(default_factory=list)\n    harness_failure_step: Optional[str] = None\n    harness_failure_exc: Optional[BaseException] = None\n\n    def record_observed(self, observed: ObservedRun) -> None:\n        self.observed = observed\n\n    def record_expect(self, result: ExpectResult) -> None:\n        self.expects.append(result)\n\n    def record_harness_failure(self, *, step: str, exc: BaseException) -> None:\n        self.harness_failure_step = step\n        self.harness_failure_exc = exc\n\n\ndef _classify_outcome(state: _LifecycleState) -> tuple[EvalStatus, Optional[str]]:\n    \"\"\"Return ``(status, error_class)`` for the captured lifecycle state.\n\n    Encodes the status-mapping rules from ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md``:\n\n    * Any captured harness exception -> ``ERRORED`` with the exception's\n      fully-qualified classname.\n    * Otherwise, all ``ExpectResult.passed == True`` -> ``PASS``.\n    * Otherwise -> ``FAIL``.\n\n    An empty ``expects`` tuple with no harness failure is ``PASS`` by\n    design (the eval ran cleanly and the manifest asked nothing of it).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if state.harness_failure_exc is not None:\n        exc = state.harness_failure_exc\n        cls = type(exc)\n        return \"ERRORED\", f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\"\n\n    if all(result.passed for result in state.expects):\n        return \"PASS\", None\n\n    return \"FAIL\", None\n\n\ndef _finalize(\n    *,\n    state: _LifecycleState,\n    home: HomeIsolation,\n    on_teardown_error: Optional[Callable[[BaseException], None]],\n    keep_home_on_pass: bool,\n) -> EvalOutcome:\n    \"\"\"Run step 7 (teardown) and build the final ``EvalOutcome``.\n\n    The ``keep`` flag follows the table in ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md``:\n    PASS honours ``keep_home_on_pass`` (default ``False``); every other\n    status forces ``keep=True`` so failed/errored evals preserve their\n    HOME for forensic inspection. Teardown failures are routed through\n    ``on_teardown_error`` so they cannot flip a PASS into a FAIL — the\n    design-spec invariant is that teardown is best-effort.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    status, error_class = _classify_outcome(state)\n\n    keep = True if status != \"PASS\" else keep_home_on_pass\n    _safe_teardown(home, keep=keep, on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error)\n\n    # ``duration_sec`` is ``0.0`` until observe completed. The observed\n    # value (which may be 0.0 for a stub eval) is preserved verbatim\n    # because the Reporter renders it without further interpretation.\n    duration_sec = state.observed.duration_sec\n    if state.harness_failure_step in {\"setup\", \"deploy_hooks\", \"spawn\", \"inject\", \"observe\"}:\n        # Steps 1-5 failures: observe never ran cleanly, so duration is\n        # not meaningful — pin to 0.0 so the Reporter's per-eval row\n        # does not display a stale or misleading value.\n        duration_sec = 0.0\n\n    return EvalOutcome(\n        eval_id=state.spec.id,\n        title=state.spec.title,\n        status=status,\n        duration_sec=duration_sec,\n        expects=tuple(state.expects),\n        skip_reason=None,\n        skip_flag_triggered=None,\n        artifacts=dict(state.observed.artifacts),\n        error_class=error_class,\n    )\n\n\ndef _safe_teardown(\n    home: HomeIsolation,\n    *,\n    keep: bool,\n    on_teardown_error: Optional[Callable[[BaseException], None]],\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Invoke ``home.teardown`` swallowing any failure.\n\n    Teardown failures are best-effort by design (see\n    ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md`` and the ``HomeIsolation.teardown``\n    docstring). The optional callback lets COMP-004 (T03.05) record\n    teardown failures into its per-eval JSONL log without re-raising\n    from the lifecycle.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    try:\n        home.teardown(keep=keep)\n    except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n        raise\n    except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — swallowed per spec\n        if on_teardown_error is not None:\n            try:\n                on_teardown_error(exc)\n            except BaseException:  # noqa: BLE001 — callback failure is non-fatal\n                pass\n","numLines":488,"startLine":1,"totalLines":488}}}

The dataclass is frozen so once a runner emits an\n281\t    outcome it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers.\n282\t\n283\t    * ``eval_id``             — id of the expanded eval. Stored as an immutable\n309\t      ``Mapping`` so the frozen dataclass keeps its hashability contract;\n310\t      an empty mapping is the default.\n311\t    * ``error_class``         — fully-qualified Python exception class name\n312\t      when ``status`` is ``ERRORED`` (e.g. ``\"builtins.RuntimeError\"``).\n313\t      ``None`` for non-error outcomes. Sequence\n        values (``requires``, ``inputs``, ``expects``, ``parameterize``) are\n        normalised to tuples; nested mappings inside ``inputs`` / ``expects``\n        / ``parameterize`` rows are passed through as-is (the schema already\n        constrained their shape).\n\n        Missing optional keys fall back to the dataclass defaults; ``id`` and\n        ``title`` are required by the schema upstream so absence here is a\n        contract bug rather than user error.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        if \"id\" not in data or \"title\" not in data:\n            raise ValueError(\n                \"EvalSpec.from_dict requires schema-validated input with 'id' and 'title'\"\n            )\n\n        requires_raw = data.get(\"requires\", ())\n        inputs_raw = data.get(\"inputs\", ())\n        expects_raw = data.get(\"expects\", ())\n        parameterize_raw = data.get(\"parameterize\", ())\n\n        return cls(\n            id=data[\"id\"],\n            title=data[\"title\"],\n            category=data.get(\"category\", \"\"),\n            requires=tuple(requires_raw),\n            timeout_sec=data.get(\"timeout_sec\"),\n            isolation=data.get(\"isolation\"),\n            inputs=tuple(inputs_raw),\n            expects=tuple(expects_raw),\n            parameterize=tuple(parameterize_raw),\n        )\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass ExpectResult:\n    \"\"\"Assertion outcome returned by every ``ExpectCallable`` (DM-009).\n\n    Six-field contract:\n\n    * ``name``         — assertion name, matching the DSL method that emitted\n      the result (e.g. ``\"exit_code\"``, ``\"file.contains_event\"``). Used by\n      the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) as a stable per-Expect key.\n    * ``passed``       — ``True`` when the assertion was satisfied. The\n      reporter rolls these up into the per-eval pass/fail bit.\n    * ``message``      — human-readable one-liner suitable for the terminal\n      summary; empty string when no message is required.\n    * ``details``      — additional structured information (typically the\n      diff fragments or matched event payload) carried through to the JSON\n      report. Stored as an immutable ``Mapping`` so the frozen dataclass\n      keeps its equality / hashability contract; an empty mapping is the\n      default for passing results.\n    * ``duration_sec`` — wall-clock seconds the assertion took to evaluate.\n      Stored as ``float`` to capture sub-millisecond resolution.\n    * ``failure``      — optional ``ExpectFailure`` (DM-005 / T01.16) carrying\n      the diff-level detail for a failing assertion. ``None`` for passing\n      results; DM-009 does not couple the failure presence to ``passed``, so\n      callers must populate ``failure`` explicitly when they want the\n      reporter to render a rich per-Expect failure block.\n\n    Equality is structural across all six fields (``@dataclass``-generated\n    ``__eq__``). The dataclass is frozen so once an ``ExpectCallable``\n    returns a result it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    name: str\n    passed: bool\n    message: str = \"\"\n    details: Mapping[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)\n    duration_sec: float = 0.0\n    failure: Optional[\"ExpectFailure\"] = None\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-009.\n\n        Uses ``dataclasses.asdict`` so nested dataclasses (notably\n        ``ExpectFailure`` once T01.16 lands) are recursively unwrapped into\n        plain dicts. ``details`` is shallow-copied to break the shared\n        reference with the dataclass instance — callers that mutate the\n        returned mapping must not affect the immutable source.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        payload = dataclasses.asdict(self)\n        # ``asdict`` already returns a fresh mapping, but ``details`` may\n        # contain nested mutable containers (lists of event payloads, etc.).\n        # We rely on ``asdict``'s recursive copy for that — no extra work\n        # needed here. Keep this method as the one stable serialisation\n        # surface so the Reporter can call it without touching internals.\n        return payload\n\n\n# Field order below mirrors DM-005 so ``to_dict()`` output is stable across\n# Reporter snapshots and review diffs.\n_EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"eval_id\",\n    \"expect_id\",\n    \"expect_name\",\n    \"expected\",\n    \"actual\",\n    \"message\",\n    \"artifact_ref\",\n    \"traceback\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass ExpectFailure:\n    \"\"\"Per-Expect failure detail record (DM-005).\n\n    The Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) attaches exactly one ``ExpectFailure``\n    to each failing assertion inside ``EvalOutcome`` (T03.01). The 8-field\n    contract intentionally mirrors the diff payload a human reviewer needs:\n\n    * ``eval_id``     — id of the eval that produced the failure (already\n      regex-guarded by FR-SCH2 / T01.05 upstream, so the reporter can use it\n      verbatim in artifact filenames).\n    * ``expect_id``   — stable per-Expect identifier such as\n      ``\"exit_code[0]\"`` or ``\"file.contains_event[2]\"``; the reporter uses\n      this to deduplicate failures across re-runs.\n    * ``expect_name`` — DSL method name that emitted the failure (matches\n      ``ExpectResult.name`` so the two records line up in the JSON report).\n    * ``expected``    — assertion's expected value as supplied by the\n      manifest. ``Any`` because primitives accept arbitrary JSON-shaped\n      payloads (int exit codes, regex strings, structured event payloads).\n    * ``actual``      — observed value the Expect saw on the run. Same\n      typing rationale as ``expected``.\n    * ``message``     — human-readable one-liner summarising the diff;\n      defaults to ``\"\"`` so a caller can omit it when the diff between\n      ``expected`` / ``actual`` is self-explanatory.\n    * ``artifact_ref`` — optional path-or-URI to a side-car artifact\n      (rendered diff file, dumped JSONL, captured stderr). ``None`` when no\n      artifact was written. The Reporter resolves the ref against the run's\n      scratch root before emitting the JSON report.\n    * ``traceback``   — optional Python traceback string captured when the\n      Expect itself raised. ``None`` for assertion failures that produced a\n      clean diff rather than an exception.\n\n    The dataclass is frozen so a Reporter consumer cannot mutate failure\n    records mid-render. Equality is structural across all 8 fields, which\n    keeps deduplication straightforward.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_id: str\n    expect_id: str\n    expect_name: str\n    expected: Any\n    actual: Any\n    message: str = \"\"\n    artifact_ref: Optional[str] = None\n    traceback: Optional[str] = None\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-005.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS`` so\n        the output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or\n        dataclass internals. ``expected`` / ``actual`` are passed through\n        as-is — callers populate them with JSON-shaped payloads, so the\n        reporter can ``json.dumps`` the result directly.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        return {name: getattr(self, name) for name in _EXPECT_FAILURE_FIELDS}\n\n\n# Field order mirrors DM-001 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` output is stable\n# across Reporter snapshots, JSON summaries, and review diffs.\n_EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"eval_id\",\n    \"title\",\n    \"status\",\n    \"duration_sec\",\n    \"expects\",\n    \"skip_reason\",\n    \"skip_flag_triggered\",\n    \"artifacts\",\n    \"error_class\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass EvalOutcome:\n    \"\"\"Per-expanded-eval outcome record emitted by ``EvalRunner`` (DM-001).\n\n    Nine-field contract consumed by the Reporter (COMP-008 / T03.13) and\n    exit-code logic. The dataclass is frozen so once a runner emits an\n    outcome it cannot be mutated by downstream consumers.\n\n    * ``eval_id``             — id of the expanded eval. Already regex-guarded\n      by FR-SCH2 (T01.05) so the Reporter can use it verbatim in artifact\n      filenames and summary keys.\n    * ``title``               — human-readable eval title (from ``EvalSpec``);\n      copied onto the outcome so the Reporter does not need to re-resolve the\n      manifest at render time.\n    * ``status``              — one of the 8 ``EvalStatus`` literals: ``PASS``,\n      ``FAIL``, ``ERRORED``, ``TIMEOUT``, ``INTERRUPTED``, ``SKIPPED``,\n      ``XFAIL``, ``XPASS``. Validated in ``__post_init__``: any other value\n      raises ``ValueError``.\n    * ``duration_sec``        — wall-clock seconds the eval ran (lifecycle\n      start to teardown). ``float`` for sub-millisecond resolution.\n    * ``expects``             — tuple of ``ExpectResult`` records, one per\n      assertion the eval evaluated. Stored as a tuple so the frozen dataclass\n      preserves equality and hashability; an empty tuple is the default for\n      ``SKIPPED`` outcomes (no assertions executed).\n    * ``skip_reason``         — free-form reason string when ``status`` is\n      ``SKIPPED`` (e.g. ``\"capability 'mcp.tavily' unresolved\"``). ``None``\n      otherwise. DM-001 does not couple ``status == SKIPPED`` to a non-None\n      reason — empty-skip rows are documented elsewhere (loader paths) and\n      the model accepts both shapes.\n    * ``skip_flag_triggered`` — name of the flag/capability that triggered\n      the skip (e.g. ``\"mcp.tavily\"``, ``\"--include-skipped\"``). ``None``\n      when the skip was not flag-driven.\n    * ``artifacts``           — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or\n      run-relative) path produced by the eval. Stored as an immutable\n      ``Mapping`` so the frozen dataclass keeps its hashability contract;\n      an empty mapping is the default.\n    * ``error_class``         — fully-qualified Python exception class name\n      when ``status`` is ``ERRORED`` (e.g. ``\"builtins.RuntimeError\"``).\n      ``None`` for non-error outcomes. The Reporter uses this to group\n      identical harness failures across runs.\n\n    Field declaration order matches DM-001 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` and\n    ``dataclasses.asdict`` produce stable, reviewable JSON.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_id: str\n    title: str\n    status: EvalStatus\n    duration_sec: float\n    expects: tuple[ExpectResult, ...] = ()\n    skip_reason: Optional[str] = None\n    skip_flag_triggered: Optional[str] = None\n    artifacts: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n    error_class: Optional[str] = None\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        # Validate status membership against the authoritative literal set.\n        # The acceptance criteria require \"Invalid status raises ValueError;\n        # valid statuses are exactly the 8 listed in DM-001\" — a runtime\n        # check is the cheapest enforcement that survives mypy being absent\n        # at runtime and callers that build outcomes from dynamic strings\n        # (e.g. JSON replay, partial summary on SIGINT).\n        if self.status not in EVAL_STATUSES:\n            raise ValueError(\n                f\"EvalOutcome.status must be one of {EVAL_STATUSES!r}; \"\n                f\"got {self.status!r}\"\n            )\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a deterministic JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-001.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS`` so the\n        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n        internals. Nested ``ExpectResult`` values are unwrapped via their\n        own ``to_dict()`` so the Reporter can ``json.dumps`` the result\n        directly without bespoke recursion. ``artifacts`` is shallow-copied\n        into a plain ``dict`` so callers that mutate the returned mapping do\n        not affect the immutable source.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        payload: dict[str, Any] = {}\n        for name in _EVAL_OUTCOME_FIELDS:\n            value = getattr(self, name)\n            if name == \"expects\":\n                payload[name] = [item.to_dict() for item in value]\n            elif name == \"artifacts\":\n                payload[name] = dict(value)\n            else:\n                payload[name] = value\n        return payload\n\n\n# Field order mirrors DM-003 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` output is stable across\n# Reporter snapshots, JSON summaries, and review diffs.\n_EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"eval_id\",\n    \"outcome\",\n    \"start\",\n    \"end\",\n    \"duration_sec\",\n    \"stdout\",\n    \"stderr\",\n    \"artifacts\",\n    \"error\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass EvalResult:\n    \"\"\"Reporter-facing per-eval result record (DM-003).\n\n    Nine-field contract consumed by COMP-008 Reporter (T03.13) as its primary\n    per-eval input. Where ``EvalOutcome`` (DM-001 / T03.01) is the *runner\n    emission* (status + assertions), ``EvalResult`` is the *reporter input*\n    that carries the captured stdout/stderr, the start/end timestamps, and the\n    harness-level error (if any). The pair travels together: runner builds an\n    ``EvalOutcome`` and wraps it in an ``EvalResult`` for the Reporter.\n\n    * ``eval_id``      — id of the expanded eval. Already regex-guarded by\n      FR-SCH2 (T01.05) upstream so the Reporter can use it verbatim in\n      artifact filenames and summary keys.\n    * ``outcome``      — the ``EvalOutcome`` emitted by the runner (DM-001).\n      Stored by-reference; the dataclass is frozen so the wrapped outcome\n      cannot be swapped after construction.\n    * ``start``        — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking the runner\n      lifecycle start. Stored as ``str`` so the field round-trips through JSON\n      without bespoke datetime serialization. The Reporter does not parse\n      these strings — it forwards them verbatim into ``summary.json``.\n    * ``end``          — ISO 8601 wall-clock timestamp marking lifecycle end\n      (teardown complete). Same string-typing rationale as ``start``.\n    * ``duration_sec`` — wall-clock seconds derived from ``end - start`` in\n      ``__post_init__``. Any value supplied by the caller is overwritten so\n      the field is *always* consistent with the timestamps. ``float`` for\n      sub-millisecond resolution. When either timestamp is empty (e.g. an\n      INTERRUPTED outcome captured before ``end`` was written) the supplied\n      value is kept verbatim so partial summaries remain constructible.\n    * ``stdout``       — captured stdout from the eval's PTY transcript.\n      ``str`` for direct JSON emission; binary noise is already stripped by\n      ``PtyStream`` (T02.17) upstream.\n    * ``stderr``       — captured stderr from the eval. Same typing rationale\n      as ``stdout``.\n    * ``artifacts``    — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or run-\n      relative) path produced by the eval. Stored as an immutable ``Mapping``\n      so the frozen dataclass keeps its hashability contract; an empty\n      mapping is the default. ``to_dict()`` returns a shallow copy so callers\n      that mutate the returned mapping do not affect the immutable source.\n    * ``error``        — optional ``BaseException`` instance captured when the\n      *harness* (not an assertion) raised. ``None`` for clean runs. The\n      Reporter renders this as ``{\"type\": \"<fqcn>\", \"message\": \"<str>\"}`` in\n      ``to_dict()``; the live exception instance is kept on the dataclass so\n      diagnostic tools can inspect the traceback if it is still attached.\n\n    Field declaration order matches DM-003 verbatim so ``to_dict()`` produces\n    stable, reviewable JSON.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_id: str\n    outcome: EvalOutcome\n    start: str\n    end: str\n    duration_sec: float = 0.0\n    stdout: str = \"\"\n    stderr: str = \"\"\n    artifacts: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n    error: Optional[BaseException] = None\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        # DM-003 acceptance: ``duration_sec`` is computed from ``end - start``\n        # consistently. We overwrite any caller-supplied value so the field\n        # cannot drift from the timestamps it derives from. Empty strings are\n        # tolerated (partial summaries on SIGINT may carry a start with no\n        # end) — in that case we keep the caller's value untouched so the\n        # Reporter can still emit a row.\n        if self.start and self.end:\n            computed = (\n                datetime.fromisoformat(self.end) - datetime.fromisoformat(self.start)\n            ).total_seconds()\n            # Frozen dataclasses block ``self.duration_sec = ...``; the\n            # documented escape hatch is ``object.__setattr__``.\n            object.__setattr__(self, \"duration_sec\", computed)\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:\n        \"\"\"Return a deterministic JSON-serialisable mapping per DM-003.\n\n        Builds an explicit ordered dict from ``_EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS`` so the\n        output ordering is stable regardless of Python version or dataclass\n        internals. ``outcome`` is unwrapped via ``EvalOutcome.to_dict()`` so\n        the Reporter never has to recurse manually. ``artifacts`` is shallow-\n        copied into a plain ``dict``. ``error`` is converted to a\n        ``{\"type\": \"<fqcn>\", \"message\": \"<str>\"}`` mapping (or ``None``) so\n        the payload remains JSON-serializable — bare ``BaseException``\n        instances cannot pass through ``json.dumps``.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        payload: dict[str, Any] = {}\n        for name in _EVAL_RESULT_FIELDS:\n            value = getattr(self, name)\n            if name == \"outcome\":\n                payload[name] = value.to_dict()\n            elif name == \"artifacts\":\n                payload[name] = dict(value)\n            elif name == \"error\":\n                payload[name] = _render_error(value)\n            else:\n                payload[name] = value\n        return payload\n\n\ndef _render_error(error: Optional[BaseException]) -> Optional[dict[str, str]]:\n    \"\"\"Render an exception as a JSON-safe mapping.\n\n    Returns ``None`` when no error was captured. Otherwise emits a two-key\n    mapping with the fully-qualified class name and the ``str()`` rendering\n    of the exception. We deliberately do *not* serialise the traceback here\n    — the per-eval JSONL log (T03.05) is the authoritative traceback source;\n    DM-003's ``error`` is the Reporter-facing summary handle.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if error is None:\n        return None\n    cls = type(error)\n    qualified = f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\"\n    return {\"type\": qualified, \"message\": str(error)}\n\n\n# Field order mirrors DM-010 verbatim so attribute traversal, factory\n# construction, and any future ``to_dict()`` consumer iterate in the same\n# canonical order the roadmap row enumerates.\n_EVAL_CONTEXT_FIELDS: tuple[str, ...] = (\n    \"eval_spec\",\n    \"home\",\n    \"home_path\",\n    \"artifacts_dir\",\n    \"run_dir\",\n    \"env\",\n    \"stdout_path\",\n    \"stderr_path\",\n    \"transcript_path\",\n    \"jsonl_paths\",\n    \"exit_code\",\n    \"stdout\",\n    \"stderr\",\n    \"duration_sec\",\n    \"artifacts\",\n)\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass EvalContext:\n    \"\"\"Runtime context passed to every ``ExpectCallable`` (DM-010).\n\n    Fifteen-field immutable view fed to each assertion primitive\n    (FR-EXP1 / T04.01) after the runner (FR-LC1 / T03.04) has finished\n    spawning the Claude subprocess and captured its outputs. Every\n    primitive ``Expect.file`` / ``Expect.jsonl`` / ``Expect.exit_code``\n    receives this exact record so manifest authors get a stable shape\n    regardless of which runner internal path produced the eval.\n\n    Field declaration order matches DM-010 verbatim (see\n    ``_EVAL_CONTEXT_FIELDS``) so structural traversal, JSON dumping by\n    downstream tools, and review diffs stay deterministic.\n\n    * ``eval_spec``       — parsed manifest row (DM-002 / T01.03). Stored\n      by-reference; ``EvalSpec`` is itself frozen so the runner cannot\n      mutate the spec after the context is built. ExpectCallables read\n      ``eval_spec.id`` / ``eval_spec.expects`` / ``eval_spec.timeout_sec``\n      directly off the context without having to re-thread the spec.\n    * ``home``            — the per-eval ``HomeIsolation`` record\n      (DM-006 / T02.04). Carries ``eval_id``, ``home_root``,\n      ``session_id``, ``time_offset_sec`` and the COMP-006 method\n      surface; ExpectCallables that need to call ``home.env()`` /\n      ``home.state_path(...)`` (e.g. ``Expect.settings_json`` reading\n      ``~/.claude/settings.json``) can do so without re-deriving the\n      object.\n    * ``home_path``       — the per-eval ``HOME`` directory created by\n      :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup`. Stored as a separate ``Path`` so\n      ExpectCallables that only need the path avoid the\n      ``home.home_path`` property indirection (and the ``RuntimeError``\n      it raises when setup has not run — by the time the context is\n      built the setup has already completed). The factory keeps\n      ``home.home_path == home_path`` invariant.\n    * ``artifacts_dir``   — directory the runner writes per-eval\n      artifacts under (PTY transcripts, hook outputs, captured\n      side-cars). Resolved beneath ``home_path``; primitives that emit\n      diff side-cars (``Expect.file`` / ``Expect.jsonl`` failures) drop\n      them here.\n    * ``run_dir``         — the run-scoped scratch directory the\n      orchestrator created (FR-G2 / T03.16). One ``run_dir`` per\n      ``superclaude eval run`` invocation; per-eval ``HOME``s and\n      artifact directories live beneath it.\n    * ``env``              — frozen mapping of environment variables that\n      were exported to the Claude subprocess (the union of\n      ``HomeIsolation.env()`` plus ``IsolationLayers.env_vars`` plus any\n      caller overrides). Stored as a ``MappingProxyType`` so callers\n      cannot mutate the record post-build; the factory wraps the supplied\n      mapping in ``MappingProxyType`` to enforce this.\n    * ``stdout_path``      — filesystem path of the captured stdout\n      transcript (the PTY-stripped variant the ``PtyStream`` / T02.17\n      wrote). ExpectCallables that need raw bytes (``Expect.stdout``)\n      open this path.\n    * ``stderr_path``      — same for stderr.\n    * ``transcript_path``  — path to the raw PTY transcript (pre-strip,\n      ANSI escapes preserved). Used by tooling that needs the byte-for-\n      byte capture; ExpectCallables generally use ``stdout_path`` /\n      ``stderr_path`` instead.\n    * ``jsonl_paths``      — frozen mapping of named JSONL log file\n      paths (e.g. ``{\"hook_log\": ..., \"telemetry\": ...}``). Per-eval\n      runtime logs land here; ``Expect.jsonl(name=\"hook_log\", ...)``\n      resolves the path through this mapping. Stored as a\n      ``MappingProxyType`` for the same immutability reason as ``env``.\n    * ``exit_code``        — captured exit code of the Claude subprocess\n      (``int``). The runner records the value the PTY driver observed\n      after ``wait()``; ``Expect.exit_code`` reads it.\n    * ``stdout``           — full captured stdout as a ``str``. Mirrors\n      what ``stdout_path`` points at so ExpectCallables can read it\n      in-memory without re-opening the file. Empty when no output was\n      captured.\n    * ``stderr``           — full captured stderr; same rationale as\n      ``stdout``.\n    * ``duration_sec``     — wall-clock seconds the eval body ran\n      (subprocess spawn to exit). ``float`` for sub-millisecond\n      resolution. ``Expect.duration`` reads this verbatim.\n    * ``artifacts``        — frozen mapping of artifact-name to absolute\n      (or run-relative) path produced by the eval. Mirrors\n      ``EvalOutcome.artifacts`` (DM-001) — the post-run inventory the\n      runner builds before emitting the outcome. Stored as a\n      ``MappingProxyType`` so the context cannot be tampered with by\n      ExpectCallables that share the record across primitives.\n\n    The dataclass is ``frozen=True`` so any attempted ``ctx.foo = bar``\n    raises :class:`dataclasses.FrozenInstanceError` — the contract every\n    ExpectCallable can rely on. Mapping fields are additionally wrapped\n    in :class:`types.MappingProxyType` to prevent the more subtle attack\n    of mutating ``ctx.env[\"HOME\"]`` (frozen-dataclass blocks\n    ``__setattr__``, not method calls on field values).\n\n    Use :meth:`from_runner_state` to construct an ``EvalContext`` from\n    the runner's internal state; direct ``EvalContext(...)`` construction\n    is permitted (tests build minimal contexts for ExpectCallable unit\n    tests) but the factory is the single place runner-internal mappings\n    are wrapped in ``MappingProxyType`` and the ``home.home_path ==\n    home_path`` invariant is enforced.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    eval_spec: \"EvalSpec\"\n    home: \"HomeIsolation\"\n    home_path: Path\n    artifacts_dir: Path\n    run_dir: Path\n    env: Mapping[str, str]\n    stdout_path: Path\n    stderr_path: Path\n    transcript_path: Path\n    jsonl_paths: Mapping[str, Path]\n    exit_code: int\n    stdout: str\n    stderr: str\n    duration_sec: float\n    artifacts: Mapping[str, str]\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        # Wrap mapping fields in MappingProxyType so callers that share\n        # the context across ExpectCallables cannot mutate the underlying\n        # mapping. Frozen-dataclass only blocks attribute assignment; the\n        # mapping objects themselves stay mutable unless we proxy them.\n        # ``object.__setattr__`` is the documented escape hatch.\n        for name in (\"env\", \"jsonl_paths\", \"artifacts\"):\n            value = getattr(self, name)\n            if not isinstance(value, MappingProxyType):\n                object.__setattr__(self, name, MappingProxyType(dict(value)))\n\n    @classmethod\n    def from_runner_state(\n        cls,\n        *,\n        eval_spec: \"EvalSpec\",\n        home: \"HomeIsolation\",\n        run_dir: Path,\n        artifacts_dir: Path,\n        stdout_path: Path,\n        stderr_path: Path,\n        transcript_path: Path,\n        jsonl_paths: Mapping[str, Path],\n        env: Mapping[str, str],\n        exit_code: int,\n        stdout: str,\n        stderr: str,\n        duration_sec: float,\n        artifacts: Mapping[str, str],\n    ) -> \"EvalContext\":\n        \"\"\"Build an ``EvalContext`` from EvalRunner internal state.\n\n        Centralises three concerns the runner would otherwise have to\n        repeat for every emitted context:\n\n        1. Resolves ``home_path`` off the ``HomeIsolation`` instance so\n           the runner does not duplicate ``home.home_path`` at the call\n           site. The property accessor raises\n           :class:`RuntimeError` if :meth:`HomeIsolation.setup` has not\n           run — which is exactly the failure we want surfaced at\n           context construction (rather than later when an\n           ExpectCallable reads ``ctx.home_path``).\n        2. Wraps the three mapping arguments (``env``, ``jsonl_paths``,\n           ``artifacts``) in :class:`types.MappingProxyType` so the\n           context's mapping fields are read-only views of the runner's\n           internal state. ``__post_init__`` would also wrap them, but\n           doing it here keeps the wrap close to the call site for\n           readability.\n        3. Produces a deterministic instance: every ``EvalContext``\n           built from the same arguments compares equal (the\n           ``@dataclass``-generated ``__eq__`` covers the 15 fields; the\n           mapping proxies compare equal when their underlying dicts do).\n\n        Keyword-only arguments are required so future field additions\n        (e.g. when DM-010 grows a 16th field) do not silently re-order\n        positional callers.\n        \"\"\"\n\n        return cls(\n            eval_spec=eval_spec,\n            home=home,\n            home_path=home.home_path,\n            artifacts_dir=artifacts_dir,\n            run_dir=run_dir,\n            env=MappingProxyType(dict(env)),\n            stdout_path=stdout_path,\n            stderr_path=stderr_path,\n            transcript_path=transcript_path,\n            jsonl_paths=MappingProxyType(dict(jsonl_paths)),\n            exit_code=exit_code,\n            stdout=stdout,\n            stderr=stderr,\n            duration_sec=duration_sec,\n            artifacts=MappingProxyType(dict(artifacts)),\n        )\n","numLines":710,"startLine":1,"totalLines":710}}}

expects tuple is PASS by design).\n* Teardown is best-effort: a failing teardown does NOT flip the status,\n  and the optional ``on_teardown_error`` callback fires instead.\n* ``KeyboardInterrupt`` / The runner *class* that\n12\tCOMP-004 (T03.05) layers on top — per-eval JSONL logging, per-eval\n13\ttimeout, parallel orchestration handoff — is explicitly out of scope here\n14\t(see §\"Out of scope for T03.04\" below).\n15\t\n16\tThe skeleton is fully testable without a real PTY: the spawn / inject /\n17\tobserve trio is injected as a `LifecycleExecutor` protocol so unit tests\n18\tsubstitute stubs that return canned exit codes and transcripts. T03.05\n19\tplugs in the concrete `PtyDriver` + `ClaudeProcessAdapter` executor.\n20\t\n21\t## 7-step lifecycle (FR-LC1)\n22\t\n23\tThe sequence below is executed verbatim by `run_eval`. Each step name\n24\tmatches the design-spec diagram (`design-spec.md §6`) and the roadmap row\n25\t(R-048) so artefact lookups stay grepable.\n26\t\n27\t| # | Step | Implementation site | Allowed exception → status |\n28\t|---|---|---|---|\n29\t| 1 | **build isolation** | `HomeIsolation.setup(config=...)` (T02.11 / D-0032). Atomic-setup wrapper (T02.13) is honoured — partial HOMEs stay on disk for forensics. | `HomeContainmentViolation` / `RuntimeError` / `OSError` → **ERRORED** (`teardown(keep=True)`). |\n30\t| 2 | **deploy hooks** | `deploy_hooks_to(home_path)` (T02.14 / D-0034). Caller-injectable so tests stub the deploy without a real `hooks.json`. | `HookDeployFailed` / `OSError` → **ERRORED** (`teardown(keep=True)`). |\n31\t| 3 | **spawn** | `executor.spawn(ctx)` — production wires `ClaudeProcessAdapter.spawn()` + `PtyDriver.spawn()` (T02.16 / T02.19). | Any `BaseException` other than `KeyboardInterrupt` / `SystemExit` → **ERRORED** (`teardown(keep=True)`). `KeyboardInterrupt` / `SystemExit` propagates so NFR-REL1 (T03.07) can mark **INTERRUPTED**. |\n32\t| 4 | **inject** | `executor.inject(ctx, prompts)` — production wires `PtyDriver.inject_prompt` and the manifest's `inputs[*]`. | Same propagation rule as step 3. |\n33\t| 5 | **observe** | `executor.observe(ctx) -> ObservedRun` — production drains `PtyDriver.read_stdout` until exit and captures `(stdout, stderr, exit_code, duration_sec, transcript_path)`. Per-eval JSONL log lives in T03.05; per-eval timeout lives in T03.07. | `TimeoutError` reserved for T03.07 → **TIMEOUT** (skeleton routes through `ERRORED` mapping today; T03.07 swaps the branch). Other exceptions → **ERRORED**. |\n34\t| 6 | **assert** | Each `ExpectCallable(ctx)` (FR-EXP1 / T04.01..T04.07 land later) is invoked. The runner builds an `EvalContext` via `EvalContext.from_runner_state(...)` (T03.03) once observe returns and passes it to every callable. | Any callable that raises → **ERRORED** with `error_class = \"<fqcn>\"`. Any callable that returns an `ExpectResult` with `passed=False` → **FAIL**. All `passed=True` → **PASS**. |\n35\t| 7 | **teardown** | `home.teardown(keep=...)`. `keep` is forced to `True` for any non-PASS outcome so failed/errored evals retain their HOME for post-mortem; PASS outcomes honour the caller's `keep_home_on_pass` (default `False`). Teardown failures are *swallowed* — they do not flip status from PASS to FAIL (the design-spec invariant: teardown is best-effort). | Logged via the optional `on_teardown_error` callback; never re-raised. |\n36\t\n37\tThe runner returns an `EvalOutcome` (DM-001 / T03.01) reflecting the\n38\tclassified status, the captured `expects` tuple, the `duration_sec` (from\n39\tthe observe step, or `0.0` when the eval errored before observe ran), and\n40\tthe `error_class` populated when status is `ERRORED`.\n41\t\n42\t## Status mapping rules\n43\t\n44\tStatus is the single field every downstream consumer (Reporter, exit code\n45\tlogic) keys off. The mapping is enforced by the private helper\n46\t`_classify_outcome(...)` so unit tests can pin the rules without\n47\tconstructing a full EvalContext.\n48\t\n49\t| Trigger | Status | `error_class` | `expects` | `duration_sec` |\n50\t|---|---|---|---|---|\n51\t| All `ExpectResult.passed == True` | **PASS** | `None` | observed tuple | observed value |\n52\t| At least one `ExpectResult.passed == False`, no harness exception | **FAIL** | `None` | observed tuple | observed value |\n53\t| Harness exception during steps 1-5 (setup, deploy, spawn, inject, observe) | **ERRORED** | fqcn of caught exception | `()` (no assertions ran) | `0.0` until observe completes, otherwise observed value |\n54\t| `ExpectCallable` itself raises during step 6 | **ERRORED** | fqcn of raising exception | partial tuple of results gathered before the raise | observed value |\n55\t| `KeyboardInterrupt` / `SystemExit` during any step | re-raised (NFR-REL1 / T03.07 marks **INTERRUPTED**) | n/a in T03.04 | n/a in T03.04 | n/a in T03.04 |\n56\t| Per-eval timeout (raised as `TimeoutError` by T03.07) | **TIMEOUT** in T03.07 — T03.04 routes through ERRORED today | fqcn `\"builtins.TimeoutError\"` | `()` | `0.0` |\n57\t\n58\t### `PASS` precondition\n59\t\n60\t`PASS` is only emitted when *every* applied `ExpectCallable` returned an\n61\t`ExpectResult` with `passed=True` and zero harness exceptions fired in\n62\tsteps 1-6. An empty `expects` tuple (manifest with `expects: []`) is\n63\ttreated as PASS by design — the eval ran cleanly and nothing was asked of\n64\tit. T04.x callable design owners can add a \"must have at least one\n65\tExpect\" loader-side guard if the suite contract requires it; the runner\n66\tdoes not police that here.\n67\t\n68\t### Why `KeyboardInterrupt` / `SystemExit` propagate\n69\t\n70\tThe atomic-setup wrapper, the executor, and `ExpectCallable` bodies\n71\tintentionally do *not* swallow these two exception types. The runner\n72\tre-raises them after running `teardown(keep=True)` so NFR-REL1 (T03.07)\n73\tcan install a signal handler that converts them into an **INTERRUPTED**\n74\toutcome at the orchestrator layer. Catching them inside the runner would\n75\tdefeat the cooperative-cancellation contract.\n76\t\n77\t## `LifecycleExecutor` protocol\n78\t\n79\t```python\n80\tclass LifecycleExecutor(Protocol):\n81\t    def spawn(self, ctx: ExecutorContext) -> None: ...\n82\t    def inject(self, ctx: ExecutorContext) -> None: ...\n83\t    def observe(self, ctx: ExecutorContext) -> ObservedRun: ...\n84\t```\n85\t\n86\t`ExecutorContext` is a small dataclass carrying the post-isolation state\n87\tthe executor needs (`home`, `home_path`, `run_dir`, `artifacts_dir`,\n88\t`stdout_path`, `stderr_path`, `transcript_path`, `env`, plus the original\n89\t`EvalSpec`). The runner *class* that\nCOMP-004 (T03.05) layers on top — per-eval JSONL logging, per-eval\ntimeout, parallel orchestration handoff — is explicitly out of scope here\n(see §\"Out of scope for T03.04\" below).\n\nThe skeleton is fully testable without a real PTY: the spawn / inject /\nobserve trio is injected as a `LifecycleExecutor` protocol so unit tests\nsubstitute stubs that return canned exit codes and transcripts. T03.05\nplugs in the concrete `PtyDriver` + `ClaudeProcessAdapter` executor.\n\n## 7-step lifecycle (FR-LC1)\n\nThe sequence below is executed verbatim by `run_eval`. Each step name\nmatches the design-spec diagram (`design-spec.md §6`) and the roadmap row\n(R-048) so artefact lookups stay grepable.\n\n| # | Step | Implementation site | Allowed exception → status |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | **build isolation** | `HomeIsolation.setup(config=...)` (T02.11 / D-0032). Atomic-setup wrapper (T02.13) is honoured — partial HOMEs stay on disk for forensics. | `HomeContainmentViolation` / `RuntimeError` / `OSError` → **ERRORED** (`teardown(keep=True)`). |\n| 2 | **deploy hooks** | `deploy_hooks_to(home_path)` (T02.14 / D-0034). Caller-injectable so tests stub the deploy without a real `hooks.json`. | `HookDeployFailed` / `OSError` → **ERRORED** (`teardown(keep=True)`). |\n| 3 | **spawn** | `executor.spawn(ctx)` — production wires `ClaudeProcessAdapter.spawn()` + `PtyDriver.spawn()` (T02.16 / T02.19). | Any `BaseException` other than `KeyboardInterrupt` / `SystemExit` → **ERRORED** (`teardown(keep=True)`). `KeyboardInterrupt` / `SystemExit` propagates so NFR-REL1 (T03.07) can mark **INTERRUPTED**. |\n| 4 | **inject** | `executor.inject(ctx, prompts)` — production wires `PtyDriver.inject_prompt` and the manifest's `inputs[*]`. | Same propagation rule as step 3. |\n| 5 | **observe** | `executor.observe(ctx) -> ObservedRun` — production drains `PtyDriver.read_stdout` until exit and captures `(stdout, stderr, exit_code, duration_sec, transcript_path)`. Per-eval JSONL log lives in T03.05; per-eval timeout lives in T03.07. | `TimeoutError` reserved for T03.07 → **TIMEOUT** (skeleton routes through `ERRORED` mapping today; T03.07 swaps the branch). Other exceptions → **ERRORED**. |\n| 6 | **assert** | Each `ExpectCallable(ctx)` (FR-EXP1 / T04.01..T04.07 land later) is invoked. The runner builds an `EvalContext` via `EvalContext.from_runner_state(...)` (T03.03) once observe returns and passes it to every callable. | Any callable that raises → **ERRORED** with `error_class = \"<fqcn>\"`. Any callable that returns an `ExpectResult` with `passed=False` → **FAIL**. All `passed=True` → **PASS**. |\n| 7 | **teardown** | `home.teardown(keep=...)`. `keep` is forced to `True` for any non-PASS outcome so failed/errored evals retain their HOME for post-mortem; PASS outcomes honour the caller's `keep_home_on_pass` (default `False`). Teardown failures are *swallowed* — they do not flip status from PASS to FAIL (the design-spec invariant: teardown is best-effort). | Logged via the optional `on_teardown_error` callback; never re-raised. |\n\nThe runner returns an `EvalOutcome` (DM-001 / T03.01) reflecting the\nclassified status, the captured `expects` tuple, the `duration_sec` (from\nthe observe step, or `0.0` when the eval errored before observe ran), and\nthe `error_class` populated when status is `ERRORED`.\n\n## Status mapping rules\n\nStatus is the single field every downstream consumer (Reporter, exit code\nlogic) keys off. The mapping is enforced by the private helper\n`_classify_outcome(...)` so unit tests can pin the rules without\nconstructing a full EvalContext.\n\n| Trigger | Status | `error_class` | `expects` | `duration_sec` |\n|---|---|---|---|---|\n| All `ExpectResult.passed == True` | **PASS** | `None` | observed tuple | observed value |\n| At least one `ExpectResult.passed == False`, no harness exception | **FAIL** | `None` | observed tuple | observed value |\n| Harness exception during steps 1-5 (setup, deploy, spawn, inject, observe) | **ERRORED** | fqcn of caught exception | `()` (no assertions ran) | `0.0` until observe completes, otherwise observed value |\n| `ExpectCallable` itself raises during step 6 | **ERRORED** | fqcn of raising exception | partial tuple of results gathered before the raise | observed value |\n| `KeyboardInterrupt` / `SystemExit` during any step | re-raised (NFR-REL1 / T03.07 marks **INTERRUPTED**) | n/a in T03.04 | n/a in T03.04 | n/a in T03.04 |\n| Per-eval timeout (raised as `TimeoutError` by T03.07) | **TIMEOUT** in T03.07 — T03.04 routes through ERRORED today | fqcn `\"builtins.TimeoutError\"` | `()` | `0.0` |\n\n### `PASS` precondition\n\n`PASS` is only emitted when *every* applied `ExpectCallable` returned an\n`ExpectResult` with `passed=True` and zero harness exceptions fired in\nsteps 1-6. An empty `expects` tuple (manifest with `expects: []`) is\ntreated as PASS by design — the eval ran cleanly and nothing was asked of\nit. T04.x callable design owners can add a \"must have at least one\nExpect\" loader-side guard if the suite contract requires it; the runner\ndoes not police that here.\n\n### Why `KeyboardInterrupt` / `SystemExit` propagate\n\nThe atomic-setup wrapper, the executor, and `ExpectCallable` bodies\nintentionally do *not* swallow these two exception types. The runner\nre-raises them after running `teardown(keep=True)` so NFR-REL1 (T03.07)\ncan install a signal handler that converts them into an **INTERRUPTED**\noutcome at the orchestrator layer. Catching them inside the runner would\ndefeat the cooperative-cancellation contract.\n\n## `LifecycleExecutor` protocol\n\n```python\nclass LifecycleExecutor(Protocol):\n    def spawn(self, ctx: ExecutorContext) -> None: ...\n    def inject(self, ctx: ExecutorContext) -> None: ...\n    def observe(self, ctx: ExecutorContext) -> ObservedRun: ...\n```\n\n`ExecutorContext` is a small dataclass carrying the post-isolation state\nthe executor needs (`home`, `home_path`, `run_dir`, `artifacts_dir`,\n`stdout_path`, `stderr_path`, `transcript_path`, `env`, plus the original\n`EvalSpec`). Tests substitute a `StubExecutor` that records the call\nsequence and returns a canned `ObservedRun`. Production code (T03.05)\nimplements the protocol on top of `PtyDriver` + `ClaudeProcessAdapter`.\n\n`ObservedRun` is a frozen dataclass carrying `(exit_code, stdout, stderr,\nduration_sec, jsonl_paths, artifacts)` — i.e. exactly the fields the\nrunner needs to build the EvalContext that step 6 will see.\n\n## Caller contract\n\n`run_eval` is called by:\n\n- **COMP-004 EvalRunner** (T03.05 / D-0049) — wraps `run_eval` in a\n  class that owns the per-eval JSONL log (`home_path/.eval-logs/`) and\n  the per-eval timeout enforcement.\n- **Unit tests** (`tests/cli/eval/test_eval_lifecycle.py`) — supply\n  stub executor + stub expect callables to pin the 7-step sequence and\n  the status mapping in isolation.\n\nThe function signature is:\n\n```python\ndef run_eval(\n    spec: EvalSpec,\n    *,\n    home: HomeIsolation,\n    config: EvalConfig,\n    run_dir: Path,\n    artifacts_dir: Path,\n    stdout_path: Path,\n    stderr_path: Path,\n    transcript_path: Path,\n    executor: LifecycleExecutor,\n    expect_callables: Sequence[ExpectCallable] = (),\n    deploy_hooks: Callable[[Path], None] = deploy_hooks_to,\n    on_teardown_error: Callable[[BaseException], None] | None = None,\n    keep_home_on_pass: bool = False,\n) -> EvalOutcome: ...\n```\n\nAll keyword-only arguments after `spec` so future field additions cannot\nsilently re-bind positional callers (mirrors the convention established\nby `EvalContext.from_runner_state` in T03.03).\n\n## Acceptance criteria → implementation map\n\n| AC bullet (T03.04) | Implementation site |\n|---|---|\n| `run_eval(spec)` executes the 7-step lifecycle and returns an `EvalOutcome`. | `run_eval` in `runner.py` — steps 1-7 land in declaration order; covered by `test_run_eval_executes_all_seven_steps_in_order`. |\n| Harness exceptions during the lifecycle produce status `ERRORED`. | `_classify_outcome` + `_run_with_classification` wrappers; covered by `test_run_eval_setup_exception_yields_errored`, `test_run_eval_deploy_hooks_exception_yields_errored`, `test_run_eval_spawn_exception_yields_errored`, `test_run_eval_expect_raise_yields_errored`. |\n| Assertion failures produce status `FAIL`. | `_classify_outcome`; Cleanup of the underlying PtyDriver\n   subprocess + zombie reaping is the contract of NFR-REL1 (T03.07);\n   T03.05 only guarantees the status mapping.\n\nThe class is a thin wrapper: every step of the seven-step lifecycle is\nstill executed by `run_eval` (the T03.04 entrypoint). The runner injects\n*logging proxies* around the user-supplied `HomeIsolation`,\n`LifecycleExecutor`, `expect_callables`, and `deploy_hooks` so the JSONL\nevents fire at the exact moment each step begins/ends without\nmodifying the lifecycle skeleton.\n\n## Public surface\n\n```python\nclass EvalRunner:\n    LOG_DIR_RELPATH: ClassVar[Path] = Path(\".eval-logs\")\n\n    EVENT_SETUP_STARTED        = \"setup_started\"\n    EVENT_SETUP_COMPLETED      = \"setup_completed\"\n    EVENT_HOOKS_DEPLOYED       = \"hooks_deployed\"\n    EVENT_SPAWN_STARTED        = \"spawn_started\"\n    EVENT_INJECT_STARTED       = \"inject_started\"\n    EVENT_OBSERVE_STARTED      = \"observe_started\"\n    EVENT_OBSERVE_COMPLETED    = \"observe_completed\"\n    EVENT_ASSERTION_STARTED    = \"assertion_started\"\n    EVENT_ASSERTION_COMPLETED  = \"assertion_completed\"\n    EVENT_TEARDOWN_STARTED     = \"teardown_started\"\n    EVENT_TEARDOWN_COMPLETED   = \"teardown_completed\"\n    EVENT_TEARDOWN_ERROR       = \"teardown_error\"\n    EVENT_TIMEOUT_FIRED        = \"timeout_fired\"\n    EVENT_OUTCOME              = \"outcome\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        *,\n        home: HomeIsolation,\n        config: EvalConfig,\n        executor: LifecycleExecutor,\n        run_dir: Path,\n        artifacts_dir: Path,\n        stdout_path: Path,\n        stderr_path: False,\n        default_timeout_sec: float | None = None,\n        clock: Callable[[], float] = time.monotonic,\n    ) -> None: ...\n\n    def run(self, spec: EvalSpec) -> EvalOutcome: ...\n```\n\nAll constructor arguments after the leading `*` are keyword-only so a\nnew lifecycle field cannot silently re-bind positional callers (the\nconvention `EvalContext.from_runner_state` and `run_eval` already\nfollow). `default_timeout_sec` is the fallback used when\n`spec.timeout_sec is None`; `None` on both means \"no timeout, run to\ncompletion.\" `clock` is monotonic by default so wall-clock skew (NTP\nadjustments mid-run) cannot produce negative deltas in the JSONL log.\n\n## Per-eval JSONL log\n\n### File location\n\n`<home_path>/.eval-logs/<eval_id>.jsonl`\n\n`home_path` is the directory `HomeIsolation.setup` materialises; the\nrunner cannot know the value until step 1 returns. Events emitted\n*before* setup completes (the `setup_started` event in particular) are\nbuffered in memory and flushed once `home_path` becomes available. If\nsetup raises before `home_path` is resolved, the buffered events are\ndiscarded — there is nowhere to write them. The Reporter does not depend\non the JSONL log; failures here never flip the EvalOutcome status.\n\n### Event line format\n\nEach line is a single JSON object. Field order is stable across runs so\ndiffing two log files of the same eval stays reviewable:\n\n```json\n{\n  \"event\": \"<event-name>\",\n  \"ts_offset_sec\": 0.012,\n  \"eval_id\": \"ExampleEval1\",\n  \"step\": \"spawn\",\n  \"extra\": { \"...\": \"...\" }\n}\n```\n\n| Field            | Type   | Notes |\n|------------------|--------|-------|\n| `event`          | string | One of the `EVENT_*` class constants. |\n| `ts_offset_sec`  | float  | Seconds since `EvalRunner.run()` began. Monotonic clock; never negative. Rounded to microseconds (`round(value, 6)`) so JSON diff output stays compact and stable. |\n| `eval_id`        | string | The eval id this log records. Already regex-guarded by FR-SCH2 upstream, so consumers can use it verbatim in filenames. |\n| `step`           | string | Coarse-grained lifecycle bucket: `\"setup\"`, `\"deploy_hooks\"`, `\"spawn\"`, `\"inject\"`, `\"observe\"`, `\"assert\"`, `\"teardown\"`, `\"timeout\"`, `\"outcome\"`. Lets consumers grep events by lifecycle phase without enumerating every event name. |\n| `extra`          | object | Event-specific payload. Always present (empty object when no extra fields). |\n\n### Required events (acceptance criterion)\n\nThe acceptance bullet pins exactly four event names — the runner emits\neach one at the documented moment. The remaining events are documented\nabove and below but are not load-bearing for T03.05 acceptance.\n\n| Event                | When                                                                                       |\n|----------------------|--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|\n| `setup_started`      | Before `home.setup()` runs. Buffered in memory until `home_path` is known.                 |\n| `spawn_started`      | Before `executor.spawn(ctx)` runs.                                                          |\n| `assertion_started`  | Before each `ExpectCallable` runs. `extra.index` carries the zero-based index; `extra.name` is the callable's `__name__` if discoverable (`None` otherwise). |\n| `teardown_started`   | Before `home.teardown(keep=...)` runs. `extra.keep` carries the resolved keep flag.        |\n\n### Extra payload contracts (informational)\n\n| Event                | `extra` keys                                                                |\n|----------------------|------------------------------------------------------------------------------|\n| `setup_started`      | (none)                                                                       |\n| `setup_completed`    | `home_path` (str)                                                            |\n| `hooks_deployed`     | (none)                                                                       |\n| `spawn_started`      | (none)                                                                       |\n| `inject_started`     | (none)                                                                       |\n| `observe_started`    | (none)                                                                       |\n| `observe_completed`  | `exit_code` (int), `duration_sec` (float)                                    |\n| `assertion_started`  | `index` (int), `name` (str | null)                                           |\n| `assertion_completed`| `index` (int), `name` (str | null), `passed` (bool)                          |\n| `teardown_started`   | `keep` (bool)                                                                |\n| `teardown_completed` | (none)                                                                       |\n| `teardown_error`     | `error_class` (str), `message` (str)                                         |\n| `timeout_fired`      | `timeout_sec` (float)                                                        |\n| `outcome`            | `status` (str), `duration_sec` (float), `error_class` (str | null)           |\n\n## Per-eval timeout\n\n### Trigger\n\n`EvalRunner.run(spec)` runs the lifecycle inside a daemon worker\nthread (`threading.Thread(daemon=True)`). The main thread joins with\n`timeout=resolved_timeout_sec`. The resolution rule:\n\n```python\nresolved_timeout_sec = spec.timeout_sec or default_timeout_sec\n```\n\nWhen `resolved_timeout_sec is None`, the join is unbounded and the\nworker runs to completion exactly as `run_eval` would have.\n\n### TIMEOUT handling\n\nWhen `thread.join(timeout=resolved_timeout_sec)` returns while\n`thread.is_alive()` is still `True`:\n\n1. The runner emits a `timeout_fired` event carrying the resolved\n   timeout value.\n2. The runner emits a `teardown_started` event with `keep=True`\n   (TIMEOUT preserves the partial HOME for forensics) and attempts a\n   best-effort `home.teardown(keep=True)` from the main thread. Failure\n   is swallowed and logged via `teardown_error`.\n3. The runner constructs and returns an `EvalOutcome` with:\n   - `status=\"TIMEOUT\"`,\n   - `duration_sec=resolved_timeout_sec` (the budget the eval used up),\n   - `expects=()` (no assertion results trusted from a half-killed\n     worker; the JSONL log retains whatever partial events the worker\n     did emit before the timeout),\n   - `error_class=\"builtins.TimeoutError\"` so downstream consumers can\n     group identical timeouts the same way they group `ERRORED` rows.\n4. The runner writes the JSONL log (including the `outcome` event) and\n   returns. The worker thread continues running detached; reaping the\n   underlying PtyDriver subprocess is the contract of NFR-REL1\n   (T03.07).\n\n### Why threads, not signal.alarm\n\n`signal.alarm` only fires on the main thread of the main process, which\nmakes it unsuitable for the parallel orchestrator (COMP-003 / T03.15\nschedules many EvalRunner workers across a `ThreadPoolExecutor`).\nThreads also let the unit tests for T03.05 exercise the timeout path\nwithout manipulating process-wide signal state. The signal-based SIGINT\n/ SIGTERM contract (cancel in-flight, mark INTERRUPTED, exit 3) is the\ncontract of T03.07 and is documented in `D-0050/spec.md`.\n\n## KeyboardInterrupt / SystemExit propagation\n\nInside the worker thread, `run_eval` already propagates these so\nNFR-REL1 can mark them as INTERRUPTED. The runner re-raises whatever\nthe worker raised by capturing it into a holder dict and re-raising on\nthe main thread after the join — the JSONL log flushes first so a Ctrl-C\nmid-run does not lose the buffered events.\n\n## Acceptance criteria → implementation map\n\n| AC bullet (T03.05) | Implementation site |\n|---|---|\n| Class `EvalRunner` exposes `run(spec) -> EvalOutcome`. | `EvalRunner.run` in `runner.py`. Covered by `test_runner_class.py::test_run_returns_eval_outcome`. |\n| Per-eval JSONL log written under `home_path/.eval-logs/` with at least `setup_started`, `spawn_started`, `assertion_started`, `teardown_started`. | `_JsonlLog` buffer + `_LoggingHomeProxy` / `_LoggingExecutor` / `_logging_expect` wrappers in `runner.py`. Covered by `test_runner_class.py::test_jsonl_log_written_with_required_events`. |\n| Per-eval timeout honoured: tasks exceeding `EvalSpec.timeout_sec` return outcome with status `TIMEOUT`. | `_run_with_timeout` thread + join contract in `runner.py`. Covered by `test_runner_class.py::test_run_returns_timeout_when_observe_hangs` and `test_runner_class.py::test_timeout_event_recorded_in_jsonl`. |\n| `artifacts/D-0049/spec.md` documents the class and logging contract. | This file. |\n\n## Module symbol re-exports\n\n`EvalRunner` is exported from `superclaude.cli.eval` so the\norchestrator (T03.15) and the integration tests can import it without\nreaching into `runner`.\n\n## Out of scope for T03.05\n\n- **NFR-REL1 signal handling** (T03.07 / D-0050) — SIGINT/SIGTERM\n  handlers, the cooperative cancellation flag, and the subprocess +\n  zombie reap on TIMEOUT.\n- **NFR-REL2 retry policy** (T03.08 / D-0051) — `retry_count=0` default\n  lives on the orchestrator.\n- **Parallel scheduling** (COMP-003 / T03.15) — `RunOrchestrator`\n  composes many `EvalRunner` instances; T03.05 only owns one.\n- **Capability-gate SKIPPED branch** — the orchestrator runs the\n  capability gate before invoking `EvalRunner.run`, so SKIPPED never\n  reaches the runner.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":323,"cache_read_input_tokens":142896,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":323,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":64,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available","speed":"standard"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"de30690a-057c-4749-af31-778837436ac5","uuid":"e3f1cf2c-100d-4e20-80d6-31c1358c5f1b"}

The resolution rule:\n\n```python\nresolved_timeout_sec = spec.timeout_sec or default_timeout_sec\n```\n\nWhen `resolved_timeout_sec is None`, the join is unbounded and the\nworker runs to completion exactly as `run_eval` would have.\n\n### TIMEOUT handling\n\nWhen `thread.join(timeout=resolved_timeout_sec)` returns while\n`thread.is_alive()` is still `True`:\n\n1. The runner emits a `timeout_fired` event carrying the resolved\n   timeout value.\n2. The runner emits a `teardown_started` event with `keep=True`\n   (TIMEOUT preserves the partial HOME for forensics) and attempts a\n   best-effort `home.teardown(keep=True)` from the main thread. Failure\n   is swallowed and logged via `teardown_error`.\n3. The runner constructs and returns an `EvalOutcome` with:\n   - `status=\"TIMEOUT\"`,\n   - `duration_sec=resolved_timeout_sec` (the budget the eval used up),\n   - `expects=()` (no assertion results trusted from a half-killed\n     worker; the JSONL log retains whatever partial events the worker\n     did emit before the timeout),\n   - `error_class=\"builtins.TimeoutError\"` so downstream consumers can\n     group identical timeouts the same way they group `ERRORED` rows.\n4. The runner writes the JSONL log (including the `outcome` event) and\n   returns. The worker thread continues running detached; reaping the\n   underlying PtyDriver subprocess is the contract of NFR-REL1\n   (T03.07).\n\n### Why threads, not signal.alarm\n\n`signal.alarm` only fires on the main thread of the main process, which\nmakes it unsuitable for the parallel orchestrator (COMP-003 / T03.15\nschedules many EvalRunner workers across a `ThreadPoolExecutor`).\nThreads also let the unit tests for T03.05 exercise the timeout path\nwithout manipulating process-wide signal state. The signal-based SIGINT\n/ SIGTERM contract (cancel in-flight, mark INTERRUPTED, exit 3) is the\ncontract of T03.07 and is documented in `D-0050/spec.md`.\n\n## KeyboardInterrupt / SystemExit propagation\n\nInside the worker thread, `run_eval` already propagates these so\nNFR-REL1 can mark them as INTERRUPTED. The runner re-raises whatever\nthe worker raised by capturing it into a holder dict and re-raising on\nthe main thread after the join — the JSONL log flushes first so a Ctrl-C\nmid-run does not lose the buffered events.\n\n## Acceptance criteria → implementation map\n\n| AC bullet (T03.05) | Implementation site |\n|---|---|\n| Class `EvalRunner` exposes `run(spec) -> EvalOutcome`. | `EvalRunner.run` in `runner.py`. Covered by `test_runner_class.py::test_run_returns_eval_outcome`. |\n| Per-eval JSONL log written under `home_path/.eval-logs/` with at least `setup_started`, `spawn_started`, `assertion_started`, `teardown_started`. | `_JsonlLog` buffer + `_LoggingHomeProxy` / `_LoggingExecutor` / `_logging_expect` wrappers in `runner.py`. Covered by `test_runner_class.py::test_jsonl_log_written_with_required_events`. |\n| Per-eval timeout honoured: tasks exceeding `EvalSpec.timeout_sec` return outcome with status `TIMEOUT`. | `_run_with_timeout` thread + join contract in `runner.py`. Covered by `test_runner_class.py::test_run_returns_timeout_when_observe_hangs` and `test_runner_class.py::test_timeout_event_recorded_in_jsonl`. |\n| `artifacts/D-0049/spec.md` documents the class and logging contract. | This file. |\n\n## Module symbol re-exports\n\n`EvalRunner` is exported from `superclaude.cli.eval` so the\norchestrator (T03.15) and the integration tests can import it without\nreaching into `runner`.\n\n## Out of scope for T03.05\n\n- **NFR-REL1 signal handling** (T03.07 / D-0050) — SIGINT/SIGTERM\n  handlers, the cooperative cancellation flag, and the subprocess +\n  zombie reap on TIMEOUT.\n- **NFR-REL2 retry policy** (T03.08 / D-0051) — `retry_count=0` default\n  lives on the orchestrator.\n- **Parallel scheduling** (COMP-003 / T03.15) — `RunOrchestrator`\n  composes many `EvalRunner` instances; T03.05 only owns one.\n- **Capability-gate SKIPPED branch** — the orchestrator runs the\n  capability gate before invoking `EvalRunner.run`, so SKIPPED never\n  reaches the runner.\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null,"userModified":false}}

Stored as an\n      immutable :class:`Mapping`.\n    * ``artifacts``    — mapping of artifact-name to absolute (or\n      run-relative) path produced by the eval. Stored as an immutable\n      :class:`Mapping`.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    exit_code: int\n    stdout: str\n    stderr: str\n    duration_sec: float\n    jsonl_paths: Mapping[str, Path] = field(default_factory=dict)\n    artifacts: Mapping[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)\n\n\nclass LifecycleExecutor(Protocol):\n    \"\"\"Strategy that performs the spawn / inject / observe steps.\n\n    Production code (T03.05) implements this on top of\n    ``ClaudeProcessAdapter`` + ``PtyDriver``. Tests substitute a stub\n    that records the call sequence and returns a canned\n    :class:`ObservedRun`. Implementations should raise on failure;\n    ``run_eval`` classifies any non-``KeyboardInterrupt`` /\n    ``SystemExit`` exception as ``ERRORED``.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def spawn(self, ctx: ExecutorContext) -> None:  # pragma: no cover - protocol\n        ...\n\n    def inject(self, ctx: ExecutorContext) -> None:  # pragma: no cover - protocol\n        ...\n\n    def observe(self, ctx: ExecutorContext) -> ObservedRun:  # pragma: no cover - protocol\n        ...\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Internal sentinel for \"no observed run yet\"\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n_NO_OBSERVED_RUN = ObservedRun(\n    exit_code=-1,\n    stdout=\"\",\n    stderr=\"\",\n    duration_sec=0.0,\n    jsonl_paths=MappingProxyType({}),\n    artifacts=MappingProxyType({}),\n)\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Public entrypoint\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef run_eval(\n    spec: EvalSpec,\n    *,\n    home: HomeIsolation,\n    config: EvalConfig,\n    run_dir: Path,\n    artifacts_dir: Path,\n    stdout_path: Path,\n    stderr_path: Path,\n    transcript_path: Path,\n    executor: LifecycleExecutor,\n    expect_callables: Sequence[ExpectCallable] = (),\n    deploy_hooks: Callable[[Path], None] = deploy_hooks_to,\n    on_teardown_error: Optional[Callable[[BaseException], None]] = None,\n    keep_home_on_pass: bool = False,\n) -> EvalOutcome:\n    \"\"\"Execute the FR-LC1 7-step lifecycle and return an ``EvalOutcome``.\n\n    The function is the *skeleton* on top of which COMP-004 EvalRunner\n    (T03.05) layers per-eval JSONL logging and per-eval timeout\n    enforcement. See ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md`` for the full lifecycle\n    + status-mapping contract.\n\n    Arguments are keyword-only (after ``spec``) so future field\n    additions cannot silently re-bind positional callers. The default\n    ``deploy_hooks`` resolves to the COMP-014 ``deploy_hooks_to``\n    function; tests inject a stub.\n\n    Raises\n    ------\n    KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit\n        Re-raised verbatim after running ``home.teardown(keep=True)`` so\n        NFR-REL1 (T03.07) can convert them into an ``INTERRUPTED``\n        outcome at the orchestrator layer. Injectable so tests stub the deploy\n        # without a real ``hooks.json`` on disk.\n        try:\n            deploy_hooks(home_path)\n        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n            raise\n        except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — captured for status mapping\n            state.record_harness_failure(step=\"deploy_hooks\", exc=exc)\n            return _finalize(\n                state=state,\n                home=home,\n                on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n                keep_home_on_pass=keep_home_on_pass,\n            )\n\n        # Build the executor's pre-observe view. ``env`` is taken from\n        # ``HomeIsolation.env()`` so the executor sees the canonical\n        # HOME / CLAUDE_SESSION_ID overlay.\n        exec_ctx = ExecutorContext(\n            eval_spec=spec,\n            home=home,\n            home_path=home_path,\n            run_dir=run_dir,\n            artifacts_dir=artifacts_dir,\n            stdout_path=stdout_path,\n            stderr_path=stderr_path,\n            transcript_path=transcript_path,\n            env=MappingProxyType(dict(home.env())),\n        )\n\n        # Step 4 — inject.\n        try:\n            executor.inject(exec_ctx)\n        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n            raise\n        except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — captured for status mapping\n            state.record_harness_failure(step=\"inject\", exc=exc)\n            return _finalize(\n                state=state,\n                home=home,\n                on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n                keep_home_on_pass=keep_home_on_pass,\n            )\n\n        # Step 5 — observe.\n        try:\n            observed = executor.observe(exec_ctx)\n        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n            raise\n        except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — captured for status mapping\n            state.record_harness_failure(step=\"observe\", exc=exc)\n            return _finalize(\n                state=state,\n                home=home,\n                on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n                keep_home_on_pass=keep_home_on_pass,\n            )\n        state.record_observed(observed)\n\n        # Step 6 — assert. Build the per-Expect EvalContext now that we\n        # have stdout/stderr/exit_code/duration, then apply each\n        # callable in declaration order. A callable that raises folds\n        # into ERRORED with a partial ``expects`` tuple (whatever\n        # callables ran before the raise).\n        ctx = EvalContext.from_runner_state(\n            eval_spec=spec,\n            home=home,\n            run_dir=run_dir,\n            artifacts_dir=artifacts_dir,\n            stdout_path=stdout_path,\n            stderr_path=stderr_path,\n            transcript_path=transcript_path,\n            jsonl_paths=observed.jsonl_paths,\n            env=exec_ctx.env,\n            exit_code=observed.exit_code,\n            stdout=observed.stdout,\n            stderr=observed.stderr,\n            duration_sec=observed.duration_sec,\n            artifacts=observed.artifacts,\n        )\n\n        for callable_ in expect_callables:\n            try:\n                result = callable_(ctx)\n            except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n                raise\n            except BaseException as exc:  # noqa: BLE001\n                state.record_harness_failure(step=\"assert\", exc=exc)\n                return _finalize(\n                    state=state,\n                    home=home,\n                    on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n                    keep_home_on_pass=keep_home_on_pass,\n                )\n            state.record_expect(result)\n\n        return _finalize(\n            state=state,\n            home=home,\n            on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error,\n            keep_home_on_pass=keep_home_on_pass,\n        )\n\n    except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):\n        # NFR-REL1 contract: cooperative cancellation. Teardown the\n        # partial HOME with ``keep=True`` so forensics survive, then\n        # re-raise so the orchestrator's signal handler can convert this\n        # into an ``INTERRUPTED`` outcome at the suite layer.\n        _safe_teardown(home, keep=True, on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error)\n        raise\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Internal state + classification helpers\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass _LifecycleState:\n    \"\"\"Mutable per-call state captured by ``run_eval``.\n\n    Kept private to the module — the public surface is ``EvalOutcome``.\n    The dataclass groups everything the classification helper needs so\n    the body of ``run_eval`` stays close to the seven-step structure\n    without smuggling positional results through ``return`` tuples.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    spec: EvalSpec\n    observed: ObservedRun = _NO_OBSERVED_RUN\n    expects: list[ExpectResult] = field(default_factory=list)\n    harness_failure_step: Optional[str] = None\n    harness_failure_exc: Optional[BaseException] = None\n\n    def record_observed(self, observed: ObservedRun) -> None:\n        self.observed = observed\n\n    def record_expect(self, result: ExpectResult) -> None:\n        self.expects.append(result)\n\n    def record_harness_failure(self, *, step: str, exc: BaseException) -> None:\n        self.harness_failure_step = step\n        self.harness_failure_exc = exc\n\n\ndef _classify_outcome(state: _LifecycleState) -> tuple[EvalStatus, Optional[str]]:\n    \"\"\"Return ``(status, error_class)`` for the captured lifecycle state.\n\n    Encodes the status-mapping rules from ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md``:\n\n    * Any captured harness exception -> ``ERRORED`` with the exception's\n      fully-qualified classname.\n    * Otherwise, all ``ExpectResult.passed == True`` -> ``PASS``.\n    * Otherwise -> ``FAIL``.\n\n    An empty ``expects`` tuple with no harness failure is ``PASS`` by\n    design (the eval ran cleanly and the manifest asked nothing of it).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if state.harness_failure_exc is not None:\n        exc = state.harness_failure_exc\n        cls = type(exc)\n        return \"ERRORED\", f\"{cls.__module__}.{cls.__qualname__}\"\n\n    if all(result.passed for result in state.expects):\n        return \"PASS\", None\n\n    return \"FAIL\", None\n\n\ndef _finalize(\n    *,\n    state: _LifecycleState,\n    home: HomeIsolation,\n    on_teardown_error: Optional[Callable[[BaseException], None]],\n    keep_home_on_pass: bool,\n) -> EvalOutcome:\n    \"\"\"Run step 7 (teardown) and build the final ``EvalOutcome``.\n\n    The ``keep`` flag follows the table in ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md``:\n    PASS honours ``keep_home_on_pass`` (default ``False``); every other\n    status forces ``keep=True`` so failed/errored evals preserve their\n    HOME for forensic inspection. Teardown failures are routed through\n    ``on_teardown_error`` so they cannot flip a PASS into a FAIL — the\n    design-spec invariant is that teardown is best-effort.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    status, error_class = _classify_outcome(state)\n\n    keep = True if status != \"PASS\" else keep_home_on_pass\n    _safe_teardown(home, keep=keep, on_teardown_error=on_teardown_error)\n\n    # ``duration_sec`` is ``0.0`` until observe completed. The observed\n    # value (which may be 0.0 for a stub eval) is preserved verbatim\n    # because the Reporter renders it without further interpretation.\n    duration_sec = state.observed.duration_sec\n    if state.harness_failure_step in {\"setup\", \"deploy_hooks\", \"spawn\", \"inject\", \"observe\"}:\n        # Steps 1-5 failures: observe never ran cleanly, so duration is\n        # not meaningful — pin to 0.0 so the Reporter's per-eval row\n        # does not display a stale or misleading value.\n        duration_sec = 0.0\n\n    return EvalOutcome(\n        eval_id=state.spec.id,\n        title=state.spec.title,\n        status=status,\n        duration_sec=duration_sec,\n        expects=tuple(state.expects),\n        skip_reason=None,\n        skip_flag_triggered=None,\n        artifacts=dict(state.observed.artifacts),\n        error_class=error_class,\n    )\n\n\ndef _safe_teardown(\n    home: HomeIsolation,\n    *,\n    keep: bool,\n    on_teardown_error: Optional[Callable[[BaseException], None]],\n) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Invoke ``home.teardown`` swallowing any failure.\n\n    Teardown failures are best-effort by design (see\n    ``artifacts/D-0048/spec.md`` and the ``HomeIsolation.teardown``\n    docstring). budget.
